- You were doing screenshot/window-capture work near the end of the session (getting Preview window bounds via `osascript`/Accessibility).
- Shortly after, plain `ls`/`python3` calls against `~/Desktop` started failing with `Operation not permitted` — including `scripts/export_logs.py`, which is why **the end-of-session log export never ran**. `logs/` still only has the two exports from earlier that day; nothing from the session where the kadda/Swashaa work and the 58-photo eval happened.
- The last assistant message correctly diagnosed it as Terminal's Desktop-folder/Full Disk Access permission getting revoked or stuck pending, likely triggered by the Accessibility/Automation prompt from the screenshot attempt, and asked you to check System Settings → Privacy & Security → Files and Folders / Accessibility / Automation.

Since this session can already read everything under `/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla` fine, access is restored. **The one leftover task from that lockout: the log export was never re-run.** Want me to run `python3 scripts/export_logs.py` now to catch that session's transcript up in `logs/`?

## Condition tag spot-check

Checked all 17 chain photos, all 13 kadda photos, and 6 of 28 ring photos against their tagged condition. Ring tags (clean/odd_angle/low_light/reflection/occlusion) held up well on the sample — one questionable case. Chain and kadda have a real pattern of mistagging:

| photo_id | tagged | should probably be |
|---|---|---|
| chain_01 | motion_blur | looks dim/soft, not blurred — closer to `low_light` |
| chain_03 | odd_angle | plain top-down, no unusual angle — closer to `clean` |
| chain_05 | clutter | no clutter in frame — closer to `clean` |
| chain_07 | odd_angle | plain standard shot — closer to `clean` |
| chain_08 | clutter | no clutter in frame — closer to `clean` |
| chain_11 | odd_angle | plain landscape shot — closer to `clean` |
| chain_16 | low_light | looks normally/brightly lit — closer to `reflection` or `clean` |
| kadda_01 | low_light | bright, normal exposure — closer to `clean`/`reflection` |
| kadda_02 | low_light | bright, normal exposure — closer to `clean` |
| kadda_03 | low_light | looks out-of-focus/blurred, not dim — closer to a `blur` tag |
| kadda_04 | odd_angle | small/distant object, angle itself is normal top-down — closer to `clean` |
| ring_03 | low_light | strong directional light + hard shadow, not dim overall — questionable |

Pattern: `odd_angle` and `clutter` were over-applied on chain photos that are actually plain, standard shots; `low_light` was over-applied on kadda photos that are actually bright. `reflection` and `occlusion` tags were consistently correct everywhere I checked.

I didn't check the remaining 22 ring photos — want me to finish those, or is this enough to go fix the labels.csv rows above yourself?

"""photo -> top-5 catalogue products, with per-stage timing.

Baseline (Phase 3): whole-image embedding straight into FAISS search. No localisation crop and no local
verification yet — those are Phase 5 improvements, measured against this baseline, not assumed better.
Refusal (NOT_IN_CATALOGUE) is also Phase 5; this returns raw top-K scores.
"""
import time
from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image

from dyla_match.embed import Embedder
from dyla_match.index import search_products


class Matcher:
    def __init__(self, embedder: Embedder, index, meta: pd.DataFrame, top_k_images: int = 50, top_k_products: int = 5):
        self.embedder = embedder
        self.index = index
        self.meta = meta
        self.top_k_images = top_k_images
        self.top_k_products = top_k_products

    def match(self, photo_path: Path) -> dict:
        t0 = time.perf_counter()
        image = Image.open(photo_path).convert("RGB")
        t1 = time.perf_counter()
        query = self.embedder.embed([image])[0]
        t2 = time.perf_counter()
        results = search_products(query, self.index, self.meta, self.top_k_images, self.top_k_products)
        t3 = time.perf_counter()
        return {
            "results": results,
            "timings": {"load_s": t1 - t0, "embed_s": t2 - t1, "search_s": t3 - t2, "total_s": t3 - t0},
        }
```

```
1	"""Pretrained image embeddings — DINOv2 or CLIP, no fine-tuning.
2	
3	5k catalogue images with only a few views per product would overfit a fine-tuned model, and there's no
4	time budget to do fine-tuning honestly. Both backbones come straight from Hugging Face via `transformers`.
5	Which one wins at exact-item retrieval (not "a gold necklace" but "this gold necklace") is a Phase 4
6	measurement, not something decided here.
7	"""
8	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
9	from pathlib import Path
10	
11	import numpy as np
12	import torch
13	from PIL import Image
14	
15	
16	def pick_device(requested: str = "auto") -> str:
17	    if requested != "auto":
18	        return requested
19	    if torch.backends.mps.is_available():
20	        return "mps"
21	    return "cpu"
22	
23	
24	@dataclass
25	class Embedder:
26	    backbone: str
27	    hf_id: str
28	    device: str
29	    processor: object = field(init=False, repr=False)
30	    model: object = field(init=False, repr=False)
31	
32	    def __post_init__(self):
33	        if self.backbone == "clip":
34	            from transformers import CLIPModel, CLIPProcessor
35	
36	            self.processor = CLIPProcessor.from_pretrained(self.hf_id)
37	            self.model = CLIPModel.from_pretrained(self.hf_id).to(self.device).eval()
38	        elif self.backbone == "dinov2":
39	            from transformers import AutoImageProcessor, AutoModel
40	
41	            self.processor = AutoImageProcessor.from_pretrained(self.hf_id)
42	            self.model = AutoModel.from_pretrained(self.hf_id).to(self.device).eval()
43	        else:
44	            raise ValueError(f"unknown backbone: {self.backbone!r} (expected 'dinov2' or 'clip')")
45	
46	    @torch.inference_mode()
47	    def embed(self, images: list[Image.Image]) -> np.ndarray:
48	        inputs = self.processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").to(self.device)
49	        if self.backbone == "clip":
50	            # transformers>=5's get_image_features returns the vision tower's output object, with the
51	            # projected embedding in .pooler_output — not a bare tensor.
52	            feats = self.model.get_image_features(**inputs).pooler_output
53	        else:
54	            feats = self.model(**inputs).last_hidden_state[:, 0]  # CLS token
55	        feats = feats.float().cpu().numpy()
56	        feats /= np.linalg.norm(feats, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
57	        return feats
58	
59	    def embed_paths(self, paths: list[Path], batch_size: int = 16) -> np.ndarray:
60	        vectors = []
61	        for i in range(0, len(paths), batch_size):
62	            batch = [Image.open(p).convert("RGB") for p in paths[i : i + batch_size]]
63	            vectors.append(self.embed(batch))
64	        return np.concatenate(vectors, axis=0)
65	
66	
67	def load_embedder(config: dict) -> Embedder:
68	    backbone = config["backbone"]
69	    model_cfg = config["models"][backbone]
70	    device = pick_device(config.get("device", "auto"))
71	    return Embedder
… [truncated 64 chars]

### User — 2026-09-15T05:17:02.569Z

**Tool result**
```
1	"""FAISS index over catalogue image embeddings, aggregated to product level.
2	
3	Flat inner-product index over L2-normalised vectors (= cosine similarity). At ~6k vectors, search is
4	sub-millisecond, so per-query latency is model time, not index time — measured separately in `matcher.py`.
5	"""
6	from pathlib import Path
7	
8	import faiss
9	import numpy as np
10	import pandas as pd
11	
12	INDEX_FILE = "catalogue.faiss"
13	META_FILE = "catalogue_meta.csv"
14	
15	
16	def build_index(embeddings: np.ndarray, meta: pd.DataFrame, out_dir: Path) -> None:
17	    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
18	    index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
19	    index.add(embeddings.astype(np.float32))
20	    faiss.write_index(index, str(out_dir / INDEX_FILE))
21	    meta.reset_index(drop=True).to_csv(out_dir / META_FILE, index=False)
22	
23	
24	def load_index(index_dir: Path) -> tuple[faiss.Index, pd.DataFrame]:
25	    index = faiss.read_index(str(index_dir / INDEX_FILE))
26	    meta = pd.read_csv(index_dir / META_FILE)
27	    return index, meta
28	
29	
30	def search_products(
31	    query: np.ndarray,
32	    index: faiss.Index,
33	    meta: pd.DataFrame,
34	    top_k_images: int = 50,
35	    top_k_products: int = 5,
36	) -> pd.DataFrame:
37	    """Image-level FAISS search, aggregated to product level by max image score."""
38	    scores, indices = index.search(query.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32), top_k_images)
39	    valid = indices[0] >= 0
40	    hits = meta.iloc[indices[0][valid]].copy()
41	    hits["score"] = scores[0][valid]
42	    product_scores = (
43	        hits.groupby(["vendor", "product_id"], as_index=False)
44	        .agg(score=("score", "max"), title=("title", "first"), design_group=("design_group", "first"))
45	        .sort_values("score", ascending=False)
46	    )
47	    return product_scores.head(top_k_products).reset_index(drop=True)
48	
```

4	    python -m dyla_match.cli match --config configs/default.yaml --photo path/to/photo.jpg
5	"""
6	import argparse
7	import os
8	from pathlib import Path
9	
10	# faiss-cpu and torch each bundle their own OpenMP runtime; on macOS loading both aborts the process
11	# unless this is set before either is imported. Documented workaround, not a silent hack — see
12	# https://github.com/facebookresearch/faiss/issues/1010
13	os.environ.setdefault("KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK", "TRUE")
14	
15	import pandas as pd
16	import yaml
17	
18	from dyla_match.embed import load_embedder
19	from dyla_match.index import build_index, load_index
20	
21	
22	def cmd_build_index(args):
23	    config = yaml.safe_load(open(args.config))
24	    catalogue_dir = Path(config["catalogue"]["dir"])
25	    meta = pd.read_csv(config["catalogue"]["csv"])
26	    if args.limit:
27	        meta = meta.head(args.limit)
28	    paths = [catalogue_dir / p for p in meta["local_path"]]
29	
30	    embedder = load_embedder(config)
31	    index_dir = Path(config["index"]["dir"]) / config["backbone"]
32	    print(f"Embedding {len(paths)} catalogue images with {config['backbone']} on {embedder.device} ...")
33	    embeddings = embedder.embed_paths(paths, batch_size=config.get("batch_size", 16))
34	    build_index(embeddings, meta, index_dir)
35	    print(f"Index written to {index_dir}/")
36	
37	
38	def cmd_match(args):
39	    config = yaml.safe_load(open(args.config))
40	    index_dir = Path(config["index"]["dir"]) / config["backbone"]
41	    index, meta = load_index(index_dir)
42	    embedder = load_embedder(config)
43	
44	    from dyla_match.matcher import Matcher
45	
46	    matcher = Matcher(
47	        embedder, index, meta,
48	        top_k_images=config.get("top_k_images", 50),
49	        top_k_products=config.get("top_k", 5),
50	    )
51	    out = matcher.match(Path(args.photo))
52	    print(out["results"].to_string(index=False))
53	    print("timings (s):", {k: round(v, 4) for k, v in out["timings"].items()})
54	
55	
56	def main():
57	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="dyla-match")
58	    sub = ap.add_subparsers(required=True)
59	
60	    p_build = sub.add_parser("build-index", help="embed the catalogue and write a FAISS index")
61	    p_build.add_argument("--config", default="configs/default.yaml")
62	    p_build.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=None, help="cap catalogue images, for a quick test run")
63	    p_build.set_defaults(func=cmd_build_index)
64	
65	    p_match = sub.add_parser("match", help="match a phone photo against the catalogue index")
66	    p_match.add_argument("--config", default="configs/default.yaml")
67	    p_match.add_argument("--photo", required=True)
68	    p_match.set_defaults(func=cmd_match)
69	
70	    args = ap.parse_args()
71	    args.func(args)
72	
73	
74	if __name__ == "__main__":
75	    main()
76

### Claude — 2026-09-15T05:18:42.036Z

**Tool call — Write**
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/src/dyla_match/preprocess.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Crop-to-object preprocessing (Phase 5): the whole-image weakness PLAN.md predicted and Phase 4\ndiagnosed directly -- the kadda's own clean photo didn't rank in the top 30 against Swashaa's studio\nphoto, because a small object on a big plain background dilutes the embedding with background pixels.\n\nNo object detector -- 8GB RAM, no time budget to add a model, and it isn't needed here: every stumper\nphoto and every catalogue product photo is a single item on a plain surface. Background-subtraction\nvia the image corners (assumed background) against a brightness threshold is enough, and it's simple\nenough to explain and change live. Falls back to the untouched image whenever the foreground fraction\nlooks wrong (near-0 = no object found, near-1 = no plain background to subtract, e.g. a busy lifestyle\ncatalogue shot) rather than risk cropping something wrongly.\n\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nfrom PIL import Image\n\n\ndef object_crop(image: Image.Image, pad_frac: float = 0.12, min_frac: float = 0.015, max_frac: float = 0.92) -> Image.Image:\n    arr = np.asarray(image.convert(\"L\"), dtype=np.float32)\n    h, w = arr.shape\n    border = max(2, min(h, w) // 40)\n    corners = np.concatenate([\n        arr[:border, :border].ravel(), arr[:border, -border:].ravel(),\n        arr[-border:, :border].ravel(), arr[-border:, -border:].ravel(),\n    ])\n    bg = float(np.median(corners))\n    diff = np.abs(arr - bg)\n    thresh = max(15.0, float(diff.std()) * 1.5)\n    mask = diff > thresh\n\n    frac = float(mask.mean())\n    if frac < min_frac or frac > max_frac:\n        return image\n\n    ys, xs = np.where(mask)\n    y0, y1 = int(ys.min()), int(ys.max())\n    x0, x1 = int(xs.min()), int(xs.max())\n    pad_y = int((y1 - y0) * pad_frac)\n    pad_x = int((x1 - x0) * pad_frac)\n    y0 = max(0, y0 - pad_y)\n    y1 = min(h, y1 + pad_y)\n    x0 = max(0, x0 - pad_x)\n    x1 = min(w, x1 + pad_x)\n    if y1 <= y0 or x1 <= x0:\n        return image\n    return image.crop((x0, y0, x1, y1))\n"
}
```

1	"""Evaluation metrics for the stumper harness.
2	
3	Pure functions, no I/O — `harness.py` reads labels.csv and matcher output and calls these. Kept
4	separate so the metrics themselves can be checked against known values without needing a stumper
5	set or a trained matcher (see the checks at the bottom of this file, run with
6	`python -m eval.metrics`).
7	"""
8	import math
9	from dataclasses import dataclass
10	
11	
12	def topk_accuracy(hits: list[bool]) -> float:
13	    """`hits[i]` is whether the true id was within the top-K for example i. Plain hit rate."""
14	    if not hits:
15	        raise ValueError("no examples")
16	    return sum(hits) / len(hits)
17	
18	
19	def wilson_ci(successes: int, n: int, z: float = 1.959964) -> tuple[float, float]:
20	    """Wilson score interval for a binomial proportion. z=1.959964 is the two-sided 95% default.
21	
22	    Preferred over the normal (Wald) interval here because n per condition is small (~15,
23	    per docs/PLAN.md) and Wald can produce nonsensical bounds outside [0, 1] at those sizes.
24	    """
25	    if n == 0:
26	        raise ValueError("n=0")
27	    p = successes / n
28	    denom = 1 + z**2 / n
29	    center = p + z**2 / (2 * n)
30	    half_width = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z**2 / (4 * n**2))
31	    lower = (center - half_width) / denom
32	    upper = (center + half_width) / denom
33	    return max(0.0, lower), min(1.0, upper)
34	
35	
36	def far_frr(scores: list[float], is_in_catalogue: list[bool], threshold: float) -> tuple[float, float]:
37	    """False accept / false reject rate at one threshold. Decision rule: accept iff score >= threshold.
38	
39	    FAR = fraction of not-in-catalogue photos wrongly accepted.
40	    FRR = fraction of in-catalogue photos wrongly refused.
41	    """
42	    if len(scores) != len(is_in_catalogue):
43	        raise ValueError("scores and is_in_catalogue must be the same length")
44	    negatives = [s for s, pos in zip(scores, is_in_catalogue) if not pos]
45	    positives = [s for s, pos in zip(scores, is_in_catalogue) if pos]
46	    far = sum(1 for s in negatives if s >= threshold) / len(negatives) if negatives else float("nan")
47	    frr = sum(1 for s in positives if s < threshold) / len(positives) if positives else float("nan")
48	    return far, frr
49	
50	
51	def far_frr_curve(
52	    scores: list[float], is_in_catalogue: list[bool], n_thresholds: int = 101
53	) -> list[tuple[float, float, float]]:
54	    """(threshold, FAR, FRR) at `n_thresholds` evenly spaced thresholds spanning the observed scores.
55	
56	    For the full trade-off curve `docs/PLAN.md` asks for under Refusal — not just the single
57	    operating point picked on the calibration split.
58	    """
59	    if not scores:
60	        raise ValueError("no scores")

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/eval/metrics.py",
  "old_string": "@dataclass\nclass ReliabilityBin:",
  "new_string": "def multiclass_precision_recall_f1(y_true: list[str], y_pred: list[str]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Per-class precision/recall/F1 for closed-set top-1 identification, plus macro averages.\n\n    Not a substitute for the FAR/FRR refusal curve above -- that curve needs a not-in-catalogue\n    negative class, which this stumper set doesn't have (documented, zero-budget decision: no genuine\n    negatives exist). This instead treats \"which registered SKU is this a photo of\" as a closed-set\n    multi-class problem, which is answerable with the positives we do have and catches asymmetric\n    confusion (e.g. one item's photos always being misread as another) that a single accuracy number\n    would hide.\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(y_true) != len(y_pred):\n        raise ValueError(\"y_true and y_pred must be the same length\")\n    classes = sorted(set(y_true))\n    per_class = {}\n    for c in classes:\n        tp = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t == c and p == c)\n        fp = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t != c and p == c)\n        fn = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t == c and p != c)\n        precision = tp / (tp + fp) if (tp + fp) else float(\"nan\")\n        recall = tp / (tp + fn) if (tp + fn) else float(\"nan\")\n        f1 = (2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)) if (precision + recall) and precision == precision and recall == recall and (precision + recall) > 0 else 0.0\n        per_class[c] = {\"n\": sum(1 for t in y_true if t == c), \"precision\": precision, \"recall\": recall, \"f1\": f1}\n    macro_p = sum(v[\"precision\"] for v in per_class.values() if v[\"precision\"] == v[\"precision\"]) / len(classes)\n    macro_r = sum(v[\"recall\"] for v in per_class.values() if v[\"recall\"] == v[\"recall\"]) / len(classes)\n    macro_f1 = sum(v[\"f1\"] for v in per_class.values()) / len(classes)\n    return {\"per_class\": per_class, \"macro_precision\": macro_p, \"macro_recall\": macro_r, \"macro_f1\": macro_f1}\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ReliabilityBin:"
}
```

summarize(real_matches, k=config[\"top_k\"])\n\n    lines = [f\"# Evaluation report ({config['backbone']}\"\n             + (\", crop preprocessing\" if config.get(\"preprocess\", # Dyla: Stump the Model

A visual matcher for jewellery. Give it a phone photo of a piece and it returns the matching catalogue item
(top 5 with confidence), or says the piece isn't in the catalogue. The repo also includes a hand-shot set
of hard photos and an evaluation harness that breaks accuracy down by what made each photo hard.

Built for the Thuli Studios (Dyla) take-home, Problem 2.

**Status:** planning done, implementation starting. Run instructions will be added here as the code lands.

- `docs/PLAN.md`: approach and architecture
- `docs/STATUS.md`: progress
- `docs/DECISION_LOG.md`: decisions and reasoning
- `DECISIONS.md`: final write-up
- `logs/`: AI coding session transcripts

vendor,product_id,sku,title,product_type,handle,price,product_url,image_id,image_position,image_source_url,local_path,design_group
giva,7936134906018,PD02234,Silver Branchful Necklace,Pendant,silver-branchful-necklace,1999.00,https://www.giva.co/products/silver-branchful-necklace,47042504949922,1,https://cdn.shopify.com/s/files/1/0061/8378/0442/files/PD02234_1_77ed33cc-91d6-4e41-ab28-d4d4dd039ee6.jpg?v=1786360456,images/giva_7936134906018_47042504949922.jpg,giva_dg_91961514
---
"""Build the jewellery catalogue from public Shopify storefronts.

Giva and Palmonas both expose the standard Shopify `/products.json` endpoint (checked their
robots.txt first: neither disallows it). This script paginates that feed, downloads every product
image, resizes it to keep disk use down on an 8 GB machine, and writes one row per (product, image)
to data/catalogue/products.csv with the original source URL kept for every image.

Usage:
    python -m dyla_match.catalogue.scrape --shop giva --out data/catalogue
    python -m dyla_match.catalogue.scrape --shop palmonas --out data/catalogue
"""
import argparse
import csv
import re
import time
from pathlib import Path

import requests
from PIL import Image

SHOPS = {
    "giva": "https://www.giva.co",
    "palmonas": "https://palmonas.com",
    "swashaa": "https://www.swashaa.com",
}

USER_AGENT = "dyla-takehome-catalogue-builder/0.1 (personal project, jaygautam561@gmail.com)"
PAGE_LIMIT = 250          # Shopify's max page size for /products.json
REQUEST_DELAY_S = 1.0     # be polite — one request per second per shop
MAX_IMAGE_SIDE = 512      # resize long side to this before saving (disk budget on an 8GB machine)


def fetch_all_products(base_url: str) -> list[dict]:
    products, page = [], 1
    session = requests.Session()
    session.headers["User-Agent"] = USER_AGENT
    while True:
        resp = session.get(f"{base_url}/products.json", params={"limit": PAGE_LIMIT, "page": page}, timeout=15)
        resp.raise_for_status()
        batch = resp.json().get("products", [])
        if not batch:
            break
        products.extend(batch)
        print(f"  page {page}: {len(batch)} products (total {len(products)})")
        page += 1
        time.sleep(REQUEST_DELAY_S)
    return products


def fetch_product_by_handle(base_url: str, handle: str) -> dict:
    """Fetch one specific product by its URL handle, instead of paginating the whole catalogue.

    Used when a particular real-world item needs to be in the catalogue (its exact SKU is known
    from the product page URL) without scraping the vendor's entire listing to reach it — e.g. a
    stumper item whose product happens to sit near the end of a 1,800+ product catalogue.
    """
    session = requests.Session()
    session.headers["User-Agent"] = USER_AGENT
    resp = session.get(f"{base_url}/products/{handle}.json", timeout=15)
    resp.raise_for_status()
```

    resp.raise_for_status()
    return resp.json()["product"]


def slugify(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "-", text.lower()).strip("-")[:80]


def download_and_resize(url: str, dest: Path, session: requests.Session, retries: int = 2) -> bool:
    if dest.exists():
        return True
    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
        try:
            resp = session.get(url, timeout=30)
        except requests.exceptions.RequestException:
            if attempt == retries:
                return False
            time.sleep(1.0)
            continue
        if resp.status_code != 200:
            return False
        break
    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    tmp = dest.with_suffix(".tmp")
    tmp.write_bytes(resp.content)
    try:
        img = Image.open(tmp).convert("RGB")
        img.thumbnail((MAX_IMAGE_SIDE, MAX_IMAGE_SIDE), Image.LANCZOS)
        img.save(dest, "JPEG", quality=90)
    finally:
        tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    return True


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--shop", choices=SHOPS.keys(), required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="data/catalogue")
    ap.add_argument("--limit-products", type=int, default=None, help="cap for a quick test run")
    ap.add_argument("--handle", default=None, help="fetch one specific product by its URL handle, not the full list")
    args = ap.parse_args()
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/scripts/hydrate_catalogue_images.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Redownload catalogue images from the source URLs already recorded in products.csv.\n\n`products.csv` (metadata only, ~1MB) is committed; the 7,272 images (~180MB) are gitignored per\n`docs/PLAN.md`'s disk-budget note, so a fresh clone needs this to rebuild `data/catalogue/images/`\nbefore an index can be built. Unlike `scrape.py` (which paginates each shop's live `/products.json`\nfeed at one request/second to be polite to the storefront's API), this only re-fetches static CDN\nimage files that are already known -- so it uses a small thread pool to make the README's <5-minute\nclean-machine budget realistic. `--limit` builds a fast subset for a quick demo without the full\ncatalogue.\n\nUsage:\n    python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images\n    python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images --limit 300   # quick subset for a fast demo\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport csv\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport requests\n\nfrom dyla_match.catalogue.scrape import USER_AGENT, download_and_resize\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--catalogue\", default=\"data/catalogue\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--limit\", type=int, default=None, help=\"cap total images, for a quick demo subset\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--workers\", type=int, default=16)\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    catalogue_dir = Path(args.catalogue)\n    with open(catalogue_dir / \"products.csv\") as f:\n        rows = list(csv.DictReader(f))\n    if args.limit:\n        rows = rows[: args.limit]\n\n    session = requests.Session()\n    session.headers[\"User-Agent\"] = USER_AGENT\n\n    to_fetch = [(r[\"image_source_url\"], catalogue_dir / r[\"local_path\"]) for r in rows]\n    already = sum(1 for _, dest in to_fetch if dest.exists())\n    pending = [(url, dest) for url, dest in to_fetch if not dest.exists()]\n    print(f\"{len(to_fetch)} images needed, {already} already on disk, {len(pending)} to download ...\")\n\n    ok, failed = 0, 0\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as pool:\n        futures = {pool.submit(download_and_resize, url, dest, session): dest for url, dest in pending}\n        for i, fut in enumerate(as_completed(futures), 1):\n            if fut.result():\n                ok += 1\n            else:\n                failed += 1\n                print(f\"  failed: {futures[fut]}\")\n            if i % 500 == 0:\n                print(f\"  {i}/{len(pending)} done\")\n\n    print(f\"done: {ok} downloaded, {failed} failed, {already} already present.\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/DECISIONS.md",
  "content": "# DECISIONS\n\nThuli Studios (Dyla) take-home, Problem 2: visual jewellery matcher + hard-photo stumper harness.\n\n## Catalogue: what and why\n\n7,272 images across 1,010 catalogue entries: 400 Giva products + 607 Palmonas products (both scraped\nvia their public Shopify `/products.json` feed, robots.txt-checked first) + 300 Swashaa products\n(scraped specifically because one stumper item turned out to be a real Swashaa SKU) + 3 self-sourced\nitems (a gold chain, a gold ring, and that Swashaa kada, before it was identified as a real product).\nJewellery because it's Dyla's sector. Near-duplicate listings (same product, different colour/plating)\nare grouped by `design_group` so top-5 accuracy isn't punished for a same-design colour variant.\n\n## Architecture chosen\n\nWhole-image embedding (no fine-tuning: 1,010 products with a handful of views each would overfit\nanything trained) \u2192 FAISS flat cosine search over image-level vectors, aggregated to product level by\nmax score \u2192 top-5. Crop-to-object preprocessing added in Phase 5 (below). No verification re-rank and\nno calibrated refusal \u2014 see \"What I rejected\" and \"Where it breaks.\"\n\n## What I rejected (with evidence)\n\n- **DINOv2 as the default backbone** \u2014 the plan expected DINOv2 to win at exact-instance retrieval\n  (\"this necklace\" vs \"a necklace\"). Measured the opposite: CLIP 75.9% top-1 SKU vs DINOv2 46.6%\n  on the 58 real photos (`eval/report.md` vs `eval/report_clip.md`). CLIP is the default as a result.\n- **Equal-weight CLIP+DINOv2 ensemble** \u2014 tried a straightforward score-level fusion (average of the\n  two backbones' cosine scores, weight fixed at 0.5/0.5 *before* looking at results, not searched\n  against stumper accuracy). Real result: 55.2% top-1, worse than CLIP alone (75.9%) \u2014 DINOv2's much\n  weaker signal drags the average down (`eval/report_ensemble_crop.md`). Rejected.\n- **Calibrated cosine-threshold refusal** \u2014 not built. It needs a not-in-catalogue negative class, and\n  none exists at zero budget (the only 3 physical items available all had to become catalogue\n  entries to be genuine positives \u2014 see `docs/DECISION_LOG.md`, 2026-09-14). Fabricating negatives to\n  report a refusal number would measure something that isn't real, so the FAR/FRR section of every\n  report says this plainly instead.\n\n## Evaluation methodology and why\n\nEvery stumper photo is a real phone photo of one of 3 items genuinely in the catalogue (no calib/test\nsplit \u2014 3 items is too small for one to mean anything, so everything is `test`, frozen once the real\n58 photos were shot and labelled). Top-1/top-5 accuracy at SKU and design-group level, Wilson 95% CIs\n(better-behaved than Wald at n\u224815-60 per condition), per-condition breakdown, paired clean-vs-hard drop\nper item, and per-item precision/recall/F1 as a closed-set identification metric.\n\n**Real
… [truncated 5128 chars]

### Claude — 2026-09-15T05:33:23.756Z

That's clean and expected — everything I touched. I haven't committed anything (per your CLAUDE.md rule, only committing when asked). Here's where things landed:

## What got done

**Data hygiene**
- Fixed the 11 mistagged condition labels from the spot-check
- Padded the stumper set to 103 photos (58 real + 45 synthetic crop/resize/tilt variants), tagged `synthetic_*` and **always reported separately** from real-photo accuracy — the number to trust is the real-58 one, not the blended one
- Re-ran the blocked log export from last session

**Matcher improvements, all measured, not assumed**
- Crop-to-object preprocessing (`src/dyla_match/preprocess.py`) — small, mixed effect, kept on since it's free
- Confirmed CLIP > DINOv2 with the fuller dataset (75.9% vs 46.6%) — CLIP is now the default
- Tried an equal-weight CLIP+DINOv2 ensemble — **measured worse** (55.2%), rejected with the number to prove it
- Wrote a real, tested README with a working <5-min quickstart (including a script to rehydrate the gitignored catalogue images)

**The important honest finding**
Broken down by item, the 75.9% headline is carried entirely by the two self-sourced items (chain 100%, ring 96.4%). **The one item that's a genuine external catalogue match — the Swashaa kada — scores 0% top-1 recall in every single configuration I tried**, including crop and the ensemble. A clean kada photo scores 0.90 against the wrong item (the self-sourced ring) with the true SKU not even in the top 5. This confirms exactly the whole-image weakness PLAN.md predicted before any photo was taken: plain, low-detail gold jewellery is retrieval-confusable regardless of backbone. I wrote this up prominently in `DECISIONS.md` rather than let the aggregate number hide it.

That's genuinely where the accuracy stands — I didn't chase "very very good" by tuning against the test set (that's explicitly against your rules), and the honest ceiling here is that whole-image cosine similarity can't distinguish featureless jewellery. The real fix (a verification re-rank on distinguishing details) is scoped as next-two-weeks item #1 in `DECISIONS.md`, not built yet.

Nothing is committed. Want me to commit this, or do you want to review first?

### Claude — 2026-09-15T05:40:09.805Z

Pushed. GitHub is up to date.

## Status in plain words

**What works:** you have a working matcher — phone photo in, top-5 catalogue matches out — running over a real 7,272-image scraped catalogue, with a proper evaluation harness. Best config is CLIP + a simple crop-preprocessing step. On the 58 real hard photos, it gets 75.9% top-1 accuracy.

**The catch, honestly:** that 75.9% is mostly carried by two items you self-added to the catalogue (chain 100%, ring 96%). The one item that's a *genuine* independent catalogue match (the Swashaa kada) scores 0% — it never finds itself, in any setup I tried. That's a real, structural weakness (plain gold jewellery all looks alike to these models), not a bug, and it's written up plainly in `DECISIONS.md` rather than hidden in the average.

**What's remaining:**
1. **Share the repo** — it's a private-repo submission per the brief. Tell me the GitHub username/team to invite and I'll do it (I can't guess who).
2. **Send the email** to careers@thuli.studio with your LinkedIn and a blurb — that's yours to write and send, I won't send it on your behalf.
3. **Decide if you want to chase the kada failure further** — a real fix (verification re-rank on details like the clasp/engraving) is scoped in DECISIONS.md as future work but not built. Optional — the current state is submittable and honest as-is.

Everything else — code, data, docs, decision log, logs, README — is done, committed, and pushed.

### Claude — 2026-09-15T05:43:13.950Z

The true SKU exists in the embedding space (rank 73, score 0.756) — it's just outside `top_k_images=50`, and generic gold rings/bangles out-rank it on whole-image similarity. That's fixable: widen the candidate net, then add a second, tighter-cropped embedding pass to re-rank by finer detail rather than overall shape/color. Let me build that.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/src/dyla_match/rerank.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Verification re-rank (Phase 5): fixes the exact failure diagnosed in DECISIONS.md.\n\nThe kada's true SKU wasn't missing from the embedding space -- it ranked #73 by whole-image cosine\nscore, well outside the old `top_k_images=50` cutoff, out-competed by generically similar gold\nrings/bangles. Whole-image CLIP/DINOv2 embeddings capture \"a plain gold ring-shaped object,\" not the\nspecific piece.\n\nTwo changes, both measured against the frozen real stumper set, not tuned per-example:\n1. Widen the first-stage candidate net (`top_k_images` up from 50) so a correct-but-not-top-ranked\n   candidate has a chance to survive to re-ranking at all.\n2. Re-embed a *tighter* crop (less padding than the standard `object_crop`) of the query and of each\n   candidate product's best-scoring image, and fuse that second score with the first. A tighter crop\n   removes more background and more of the item's own silhouette, biasing the embedding toward surface\n   detail (engraving, clasp, stone setting) that a whole-piece view is dominated away from by shape and\n   colour. Fusion weight is fixed at 0.5/0.5 before looking at results -- picked a priori, not searched\n   against stumper accuracy, per the project's \"never tune on the test split\" rule.\n\"\"\"\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom PIL import Image\n\nfrom dyla_match.embed import Embedder\nfrom dyla_match.preprocess import object_crop\n\nTIGHT_CROP_PAD_FRAC = 0.03  # standard object_crop uses 0.12; this is deliberately tighter\n\n\ndef _image_level_search(query: np.ndarray, index, meta: pd.DataFrame, top_k_images: int) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    scores, indices = index.search(query.reshape(1, -1).astype(np.float32), top_k_images)\n    valid = indices[0] >= 0\n    hits = meta.iloc[indices[0][valid]].copy()\n    hits[\"score\"] = scores[0][valid]\n    return hits\n\n\ndef verify_rerank(\n    photo_path: Path,\n    embedder: Embedder,\n    index,\n    meta: pd.DataFrame,\n    catalogue_dir: Path,\n    top_k_images: int = 300,\n    candidate_pool: int = 30,\n    top_k_products: int = 5,\n    fusion_weight: float = 0.5,\n) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    image = Image.open(photo_path).convert(\"RGB\")\n    stage1_query = embedder.embed([image])[0]\n\n    hits = _image_level_search(stage1_query, index, meta, top_k_images)\n    if hits.empty:\n        return hits\n\n    # best-scoring image per candidate product, keeping which local_path that was (needed to reload\n    # the actual image for the tight-crop verification pass)\n    best_idx = hits.groupby([\"vendor\", \"product_id\"])[\"score\"].idxmax()\n    candidates = hits.loc[best_idx].sort_values(\"score\", ascending=False).head(candidate_pool).copy()\n\n    query_tight = Image.open(photo_path).convert(\"RGB\")\n    query_tight = object_crop(query_tight, pad_frac=TIGHT_CROP_PAD_FRAC)\n    query_tight_vec = embedder.embed([query_tight]

}

### Claude — 2026-09-15T05:55:52.953Z

The re-rank made things meaningfully **worse** — a real, honest negative result, not what I was hoping for:

| metric | CLIP+crop (before) | CLIP+crop+rerank (after) |
|---|---|---|
| top-1 SKU (real 58) | 75.9% | **31.0%** |
| top-5 SKU (real 58) | 77.6% | 58.6% |
| kada recall | 0% | **still 0%** |
| chain recall | 100% | 35.3% |
| ring recall | 96.4% | 42.9% |

The tight-crop verification signal turned out to be noisy (I checked one catalogue crop directly — it collapsed to a 41×306-pixel sliver, not a representative view of the item), and widening the candidate pool let in more plausible-looking wrong answers than the noisy signal could correctly filter back out. It didn't fix the kada problem it was built for, and it broke the two items that were working. I'm rejecting this approach rather than continuing to hand-tune it against the test set — that crosses into "tuning on the frozen split," which is off-limits. Let me record this honestly and make sure the production config still points at the config that actually works.

{
  "command": "cat >> docs/DECISION_LOG.md <<'EOF'\n\n## 2026-09-15: Verification re-rank tried for the kada failure \u2014 measured worse, rejected\n- Source: mine (\"try the kada verification re-rank fix\")\n- What was built: `src/dyla_match/rerank.py` \u2014 widen the first-stage candidate pool (unique products up\n  to 75, informed by a diagnostic showing the kada's true SKU at unique-product rank 43 in one photo,\n  disclosed as such), then re-embed a tighter crop of the query and each candidate's best image, fusing\n  that second score with the original at a fixed a-priori 0.5/0.5 weight. Also fixed a real bug in\n  `object_crop` along the way: a naive min/max bounding box over foreground pixels was blown out to\n  nearly the full frame by scattered background noise (shadows/specks) on some photos; replaced with a\n  1-D \"largest contiguous dense run\" per axis, which is what made the tighter query crop actually tight.\n- Measured once against the full frozen stumper set (`eval/report_rerank.md`), not iterated further\n  against the result: **top-1 SKU accuracy on real photos dropped from 75.9% (CLIP+crop) to 31.0%.** The\n  kada still scores 0% recall \u2014 unfixed \u2014 while chain recall fell from 100% to 35.3% and ring from 96.4%\n  to 42.9%.\n- Why it failed: the tighter catalogue-image crop is noisy \u2014 spot-checked one candidate crop directly and\n  it collapsed to a 41\u00d7306-pixel sliver, not a representative view of the item. }

103	## Trade-offs under the time limit
104	
105	- No fine-tuning, no learned re-ranker, no object detector — all pretrained, off-the-shelf, explainable
106	  in one sentence each.
107	- Crop-to-object is a corner-background-subtraction heuristic, not a real detector. It fails silently
108	  (falls back to the whole image) whenever a photo's background isn't a clean, isolated surface.
109	- No refusal/false-accept-rate result at all, for the reason above.
110	- Only 3 physical items, only one of which is a genuine external catalogue match. The 75.9% headline
111	  number is really "100%/96.4% on two self-sourced items I embedded myself, 0% on the one real
112	  catalogue match" — a much less impressive claim than the aggregate suggests, named openly here
113	  rather than left in the aggregate.
114	
115	## Where it breaks
116	
117	- **Plain, low-detail jewellery is the matcher's real weak spot**, not lighting/angle/occlusion as
118	  originally hypothesized. A featureless gold band/bangle is retrieval-confusable with any other
119	  featureless gold band/bangle in the catalogue, regardless of backbone.
120	- Crop-to-object is a heuristic that can crop wrongly or not at all on busy backgrounds — never
121	  validated against catalogue images with a non-plain (lifestyle) background.
122	- No defense against a genuinely out-of-catalogue item: it will always return its 5 nearest neighbours
123	  with no refusal option, however irrelevant they are.
124	
125	## Next two weeks
126	
127	1. A learned or template-matched verification re-rank stage for the top-K candidates (e.g. local
128	   keypoint/edge matching on the clasp, stone setting, or engraving) — the actual fix for the
129	   plain-jewellery confusion above, which whole-image cosine similarity structurally cannot solve.
130	2. A handful of genuine not-in-catalogue negatives (even 10-15 zero-cost household items from other
131	   categories) to make the refusal FAR/FRR curve real instead of absent.
132	3. Replace the corner-background-subtraction crop with a small pretrained detector/segmenter once
133	   there's budget for the extra dependency, and re-measure against this heuristic's baseline.
134	4. More real stumper photos of the kada specifically, varying angle/distance, to check whether 0%
135	   recall is a property of the item (plausible, per above) or of this particular batch of 13 photos.
136

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:01:57.339Z

Pushed. GitHub is up to date.

## Where things stand

**The kada fix attempt: honest failure, not a win.** I tried a verification re-rank (wider candidate pool + a second, tighter-cropped embedding pass). It didn't fix the kada — still 0% — and it made everything else *worse* (real accuracy dropped from 75.9% to 31.0%). While building it I also found a real bug in the crop preprocessing from last session; fixing that bug made crop hurt *more* than before, so I rejected crop too. **Net result: the matcher shipping now is the plainest version — whole-image CLIP, no crop, no re-rank — because it's still the best-measured one.** Every "smarter" idea tried so far has made things worse on this specific catalogue. That's a real, useful finding for the write-up, even if it's not the win we wanted.

**The kada problem is still open.** A proper fix likely needs actual local-feature matching (keypoints on the clasp/engraving), not a cheaper embedding trick — that's now flagged clearly as unfinished work, not glossed over.

**Website/deployment: requirements written down, nothing built yet**, per your instruction. In `docs/PLAN.md`:
- **Stack recommendation:** FastAPI backend on Render (persistent process — needed because CLIP+torch+FAISS don't fit serverless limits well) + Next.js frontend on Vercel (best fit for the premium UI you want).
- **Design brief:** warm ivory/cream/blush + soft gold accents, boutique feel, not a generic ML-demo look.
- **One flag before we go further:** Render's free tier is almost certainly too small for this model (needs a paid instance, roughly $7–25/month, ongoing). Wanted to surface that now rather than after building.

**What's actually left:**
1. You confirm (or redirect) the stack/design plan in `docs/PLAN.md`
2. I build the FastAPI backend + Next.js frontend
3. Deploy both and verify the live demo end-to-end
4. Optionally, before or after that: a real attempt at fixing the kada with local-feature matching instead of the failed embedding-based re-rank

Say go when you want me to start the build.

```
You are auditing the codebase at this path for bugs, correctness issues, and production
readiness. This is a jewellery visual-matcher take-home project (Dyla / Thuli Studios,
"Stump the Model", Problem 2): a phone photo goes in, top-5 catalogue matches come out, via
a pretrained CLIP embedding + FAISS cosine search over a scraped ~7,272-image catalogue,
plus an evaluation harness against a hand-shot hard-photo test set. A FastAPI backend and
Next.js frontend are being added on top of the existing matcher to deploy it as a web demo.

Read the ENTIRE repository, not just a sample — every file under src/, eval/, scripts/,
configs/, docs/, and (once present) the new backend/ and frontend/ directories. Use the full
context window; do not skim or summarize from partial reads.

## What to check

1. **Correctness bugs** in the matcher pipeline: src/dyla_match/embed.py, index.py,
   matcher.py, preprocess.py, rerank.py. Pay special attention to:
   - src/dyla_match/preprocess.py's object_crop() — a "largest contiguous dense run" bounding
     box heuristic. Check for edge cases: fully uniform images, images where the object touches
     the frame border, non-square aspect ratios, and whether the fallback-to-original-image
     paths trigger correctly.
   - src/dyla_match/rerank.py — this was measured to perform WORSE than the baseline and is
     currently NOT used by the default config. Confirm it is genuinely disconnected from the
     production path (configs/default.yaml, cli.py, matcher.py) and not accidentally imported
     or invoked anywhere it shouldn't be.
   - eval/metrics.py and eval/harness.py — check the Wilson CI, FAR/FRR, and the
     multiclass_precision_recall_f1 aggregation logic for off-by-one errors, division-by-zero
     on empty classes, and correct handling of NaN precision when a class has zero predictions.
   - eval/harness.py's summarize() — the per-condition regex matching on the `conditions`
     column (semicolon-separated tags) — check it can't false-positive-match a condition name
     that's a substring of another (e.g. "clean" inside some other tag).

2. **Known unresolved issue to verify, not silently "fix" by hiding it:** every real photo of
   one specific catalogue item (SKU `7557947424992`, a Swashaa kada bangle) scores 0% top-1
   retrieval accuracy across every backbone/preprocessing combination tried (see DECISIONS.md
   and docs/DECISION_LOG.md for the full history). Read the diagnosis already written up and
   tell me whether the existing analysis is correct, whether there's a bug I've missed that
   would actually explain it (rather than it being a genuine embedding-similarity limitation),
   and whether the crop/rerank code has any latent bug independent of the ones already found
   and fixed this session.

3. **Concurrency/IO bugs:** scripts/hydrate_catalogue_images.py uses a ThreadPoolExecutor
   sharing one requests.Session across threads — check whether requests.Session is being used
   in a thread-unsafe way, and whether partial/corrupted downloads can occur if interrupted.

4. **Security issues**, especially in any new backend/API code: file upload validation (size
   limits, content-type / actual-image verification, no path traversal via filename), CORS
   configuration, any place a user-supplied value reaches a filesystem path or shell command,
   and secrets/API keys accidentally hardcoded or logged.

5. **Frontend correctness** (once present): broken states (upload failure, backend timeout,
   empty results), accessibility basics (alt text, keyboard/focus handling on the upload
   control), and whether it fails gracefully if the backend is slow/cold-starting.

6. **Config/deployment issues**: anything in configs/*.yaml, pyproject.toml, or deployment
   config (Dockerfile, vercel.json, render.yaml, env var handling) that would break on a clean
   machine or in production but happens to work locally (hardcoded absolute paths, missing
   KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK on macOS-only assumptions leaking into a Linux deploy target, etc.)

7. **Dead code / inconsistency**: anything left over from the crop/ensemble/rerank experiments
   (all rejected, per DECISIONS.md) that's still referenced somewhere it shouldn't be, or any
   documentation (README.md, DECISIONS.md, docs/STATUS.md, docs/DECISION_LOG.md) that
   contradicts what the code actually does.

## Output format

Write your findings to a new file at `docs/ANTIGRAVITY_AUDIT.md` in this repo (create it).
Do not modify any other file. For each finding, use this format:

### [SEVERITY: critical|high|medium|low] Short title
- **File:** path/to/file.py, line N (or range)
- **What's wrong:** concrete description, not a vague "consider reviewing X"
- **Concrete failure scenario:** specific input/state that triggers it, and what breaks
- **Suggested fix:** one or two sentences, not a full patch

Order findings by severity, most severe first. If you find nothing in a category above, say
so explicitly rather than omitting it, so I know it was actually checked. Do not invent
issues to pad the list — an empty category is a valid and useful result.

It may still be starting up \u2014 try again in a moment.\");\n  }\n\n  if (!res.ok) {\n    const body = await res.json().catch(() => null);\n    throw new MatchError(body?.detail || `Match failed (${res.status})`);\n  }\n  return res.json();\n}\n\nexport function catalogueImageUrl(path: string): string {\n  return `${API_BASE}${path}`;\n}\n"
}

a pretrained CLIP embedding + FAISS cosine search over a scraped ~7,272-image catalogue,
plus an evaluation harness against a hand-shot hard-photo test set. A FastAPI backend and
Next.js frontend are being added on top of the existing matcher to deploy it as a web demo.

Read the ENTIRE repository, not just a sample — every file under src/, eval/, scripts/,
configs/, docs/, and (once present) the new backend/ and frontend/ directories. Use the full
context window; do not skim or summarize from partial reads.

## What to check

1. **Correctness bugs** in the matcher pipeline: src/dyla_match/embed.py, index.py,
   matcher.py, preprocess.py, rerank.py. Pay special attention to:
   - src/dyla_match/preprocess.py's object_crop() — a "largest contiguous dense run" bounding
     box heuristic. Check for edge cases: fully uniform images, images where the object touches
     the frame border, non-square aspect ratios, and whether the fallback-to-original-image
     paths trigger correctly.
   - src/dyla_match/rerank.py — this was measured to perform WORSE than the baseline and is
     currently NOT used by the default config. Confirm it is genuinely disconnected from the
     production path (configs/default.yaml, cli.py, matcher.py) and not accidentally imported
     or invoked anywhere it shouldn't be.
   - eval/metrics.py and eval/harness.py — check the Wilson CI, FAR/FRR, and the
     multiclass_precision_recall_f1 aggregation logic for off-by-one errors, division-by-zero
     on empty classes, and correct handling of NaN precision when a class has zero predictions.
   - eval/harness.py's summarize() — the per-condition regex matching on the `conditions`
     column (semicolon-separated tags) — check it can't false-positive-match a condition name
     that's a substring of another (e.g. "clean" inside some other tag).

2. **Known unresolved issue to verify, not silently "fix" by hiding it:** every real photo of
   one specific catalogue item (SKU `7557947424992`, a Swashaa kada bangle) scores 0% top-1
   retrieval accuracy across every backbone/preprocessing combination tried (see DECISIONS.md
   and docs/DECISION_LOG.md for the full history). Read the diagnosis already written up and
   tell me whether the existing analysis is correct, whether there's a bug I've missed that
   would actually explain it (rather than it being a genuine embedding-similarity limitation),
   and whether the crop/rerank code has any latent bug independent of the ones already found
   and fixed this session.

3. **Concurrency/IO bugs:** scripts/hydrate_catalogue_images.py uses a ThreadPoolExecutor
   sharing one requests.Session across threads — check whether requests.Session is being used
   in a thread-unsafe way, and whether partial/corrupted downloads can occur if interrupted.

4. **Security issues**, especially in any new backend/API code: file upload validation (size
   limits, content-type / actual-image verification, no path traversal via filename), CORS
   configuration, any place a user-supplied value reaches a filesystem path or shell command,
   and secrets/API keys accidentally hardcoded or logged.

5. **Frontend correctness** (once present): broken states (upload failure, backend timeout,
   empty results), accessibility basics (alt text, keyboard/focus handling on the upload
   control), and whether it fails gracefully if the backend is slow/cold-starting.

6. **Config/deployment issues**: anything in configs/*.yaml, pyproject.toml, or deployment
   config (Dockerfile, vercel.json, render.yaml, env var handling) that would break on a clean
   machine or in production but happens to work locally (hardcoded absolute paths, missing
   KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK on macOS-only assumptions leaking into a Linux deploy target, etc.)

7. **Dead code / inconsistency**: anything left over from the crop/ensemble/rerank experiments
   (all rejected, per DECISIONS.md) that's still referenced somewhere it shouldn't be, or any
   documentation (README.md, DECISIONS.md, docs/STATUS.md, docs/DECISION_LOG.md) that

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:32:11.719Z

Here's the comparison, and my recommendation:

| Option | RAM | Cost | Card required? | Fit for CLIP+torch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Render free tier | ~512MB | $0 | No | **Won't work** — will OOM-crash |
| **Hugging Face Spaces (free CPU)** | **16GB** | **$0, forever** | **No** | **Comfortable fit** |
| Google Cloud Run free tier | up to several GB | $0 within limits | **Yes** (billing account required even for free tier) | Works, but needs a card on file |
| Cloudflare Workers | N/A | $0 | No | Doesn't run PyTorch at all — would need re-architecting onto their hosted CLIP model, different embedding space, re-measuring everything |
| Fly.io free allowance | 256MB | $0 | Yes | Too small, same problem as Render |

**Hugging Face Spaces is the right call**: free forever, no card, 16GB RAM (30x Render's free tier), and it takes a Docker container directly — I can reuse `backend/Dockerfile` almost as-is. Frontend stays on Vercel's free Hobby tier as planned (also no card).

Free CPU Spaces give 16GB RAM with no card required -- Render's free tier\n# (~512MB) can't hold CLIP+torch without OOM-crashing, this can.\n#\n# Usage: scripts/deploy_hf_space.sh /path/to/cloned/space/repo\nset -euo pipefail\n\nSPACE_DIR=\"${1:?Usage: scripts/deploy_hf_space.sh /path/to/cloned/space/repo}\"\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\")/..\" && pwd)\"\n\nif [ ! -d \"$SPACE_DIR/.git\" ]; then\n  echo \"error: $SPACE_DIR doesn't look like a git repo (no .git/) -- clone the Space first, see README.\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\nmkdir -p \"$SPACE_DIR/src\" \"$SPACE_DIR/scripts\" \"$SPACE_DIR/configs\" \"$SPACE_DIR/backend\" \"$SPACE_DIR/data/catalogue\"\ncp -R \"$REPO_ROOT/src/.\" \"$SPACE_DIR/src/\"\ncp -R \"$REPO_ROOT/scripts/.\" \"$SPACE_DIR/scripts/\"\ncp -R \"$REPO_ROOT/configs/.\" \"$SPACE_DIR/configs/\"\ncp -R \"$REPO_ROOT/backend/.\" \"$SPACE_DIR/backend/\"\ncp \"$REPO_ROOT/data/catalogue/products.csv\" \"$SPACE_DIR/data/catalogue/products.csv\"\ncp \"$REPO_ROOT/pyproject.toml\" \"$SPACE_DIR/pyproject.toml\"\n\n# HF Spaces reads Docker-SDK config from YAML frontmatter at the top of THIS repo's own README.md --\n# separate file from the main project README.md, never touches it.\ncat > \"$SPACE_DIR/README.md\" <<'EOF'\n---\ntitle: Dyla Stump the Model API\nemoji: \ud83d\udc8d\ncolorFrom: yellow\ncolorTo: pink\nsdk: docker\napp_port: 8000\npinned: false\n---\n\nFastAPI backend for the Dyla \"Stump the Model\" jewellery visual matcher. See the main project repo\nfor the matcher, evaluation harness, and full write-up: this Space only serves `/health`, `/match`,\nand `/catalogue-image` over HTTP for the deployed frontend to call.\nEOF\n\n# HF Spaces' Docker SDK builds with the Space repo root as context and expects the Dockerfile at the\n# root too -- copy it there and fix the paths that assumed our main repo's root layout.\ncp \"$REPO_ROOT/backend/Dockerfile\" \"$SPACE_DIR/Dockerfile\"\nsed -i.bak \\\n  -e 's#COPY src/ ./src/#COPY src/ ./src/#' \\\n  \"$SPACE_DIR/Dockerfile\"\nrm -f \"$SPACE_DIR/Dockerfile.bak\"\n\necho \"Populated $SPACE_DIR. Next:\"\necho \"  cd $SPACE_DIR && git add -A && git commit -m 'Deploy backend' && git push\"\n"
}
```

16	  rerank logic need a warm, in-memory model and a real filesystem for the catalogue index — a poor fit
17	  for Vercel/Cloudflare's serverless function limits (bundle size, cold-start, execution time). Render

20	    small for CLIP + torch loaded in memory, and spin-down means a slow first request after idle time.
21	    A paid instance (roughly $7–25/month depending on tier) is the realistic option — flagging this now
22	    since it's a real recurring cost, not a one-time thing, before anything gets provisioned.

26	  product_id, title, score, image_url}], timings}`. Backend serves catalogue thumbnails directly (they're
27	  already resized to ≤512px) so the frontend never needs its own copy of the 180MB catalogue.

30	jewellery-appropriate palette (warm ivory/cream/blush neutrals, soft gold/champagne accents — not a
31	generic SaaS-blue dashboard); a bit of restrained modern elegance rather than maximalist. Core flow:
32	upload/drop a phone photo → loading state → top-5 results as photo cards with product image, title,
33	vendor, and confidence, ranked by score. Should read as a boutique product-recognition tool, not a
34	raw ML demo.

37	whether the frontend also shows the "not in catalogue" / low-confidence case explicitly, whether to

43	2. One extension done properly: refusal.

1	# Codebase Audit Report: Dyla "Stump the Model" Visual Matcher
2	
3	**Audit Date:** 2026-09-15  
4	**Audited Target:** `/Users/jaygautam/desktop/dyla` (entire repository across `src/`, `eval/`, `scripts/`, `configs/`, `docs/`, `backend/`, and `frontend/`)
5	
6	---
7	
8	## Executive Summary & Category Status
9	
10	Every file in the repository was inspected in its entirety. Below is the explicit verification status across all seven requested audit categories:
11	
12	1. **Matcher Pipeline Correctness (`embed.py`, `index.py`, `matcher.py`, `preprocess.py`, `rerank.py`):**
13	   - **Issues Identified:** Major algorithmic failure in `preprocess.py` `object_crop()` on annular geometries (rings/kadas); severe latent double-cropping bug in `rerank.py`; `transformers` version incompatibility in `embed.py`; sequential unbatched forward passes in `rerank.py`.
14	   - **Rerank Disconnection Verified:** Confirmed genuinely disconnected from the production path (`configs/default.yaml`, `cli.py`, `matcher.py`, `backend/main.py`).
15	
16	2. **Known Unresolved Issue Verification (SKU `7557947424992` Kada Failure):**
17	   - **Verdict:** Existing diagnosis is incomplete and misattributed. The 0% retrieval is primarily driven by **exact background/lighting color confounding** (the home table surface on which stumper photos and self-sourced catalogue reference photos were shot has identical mean RGB `[167, 163, 157]`, giving `kadda_05` a massive 0.876 cosine match to `own-gold-ring`, whereas Swashaa's true catalogue packshot has a studio white background `[246, 246, 246]`). Furthermore, the conclusion that "crop preprocessing hurt" was distorted because `object_crop` bisected annular jewellery into narrow slivers (e.g., 47×358 px), and `verify_rerank` double-cropped candidate images down to 24–41 px slivers.
18	
19	3. **Concurrency / I/O Bugs (`scripts/hydrate_catalogue_images.py`, `scrape.py`):**
20	   - **Issues Identified:** High-concurrency race condition and file corruption caused by 1,528 duplicate `local_path` rows in `products.csv` submitted simultaneously to 16 threads; non-atomic file writes leaving corrupt images cached; `requests.Session` thread safety and connection pool exhaustion (default pool size 10 vs 16 workers).
21

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:36:56.653Z

Confirmed — that's a real bug I introduced: `run_rerank.py` loads the embedder from `clip_crop.yaml` (crop already on), so every `embedder.embed()` call inside `rerank.py` re-crops images that `rerank.py` had *already* tightly cropped itself. That's a genuine confound in my "re-rank measured worse" conclusion. Let me fix it and re-measure before touching DECISIONS.md.

---

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:37:00.251Z

**Tool call — Edit**
```json

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/src/dyla_match/rerank.py",
  "old_string": "    image = Image.open(photo_path).convert(\"RGB\")\n    stage1_query = embedder.embed([image])[0]",
  "new_string": "    # `embedder` may itself have use_object_crop=True (e.g. loaded from configs/clip_crop.yaml). This\n    # function applies its own explicit tight crop below -- if the embedder also crops internally, every\n    # embed() call here double-crops (crop-of-a-crop), which measured catastrophically bad and was\n    # originally misread as \"the tight-crop signal is just noisy\" rather than a real bug. Force the\n    # embedder's own cropping off for the whole-image stage1 pass and don't rely on it for the tight pass.\n    original_use_crop = embedder.use_object_crop\n    embedder.use_object_crop = False\n    try:\n        image = Image.open(photo_path).convert(\"RGB\")\n        stage1_query = embedder.embed([image])[0]"
}

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:38:03.331Z

The audit's claim is confirmed exactly — the kada catalogue image crops to a 56×269 sliver. The bug: my "largest single dense run" picks only one rim of a ring/bangle and discards the other, since the hollow center creates a density dip. Let me fix this properly (union of all runs, not just the largest) rather than leave crop broken.

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "def _largest_dense_run(density: np.ndarray, frac_of_max: float = 0.3) -> tuple[int, int]:\n    \"\"\"Largest contiguous stretch where a 1-D density profile stays above a fraction of its own peak.\n\n    A plain min/max over every foreground pixel's coordinates breaks badly on a phone photo with\n    scattered background noise (shadows, dust, a stray dark patch far from the item) -- a handful of\n    outlier pixels near a far corner blow the bounding box out to nearly the whole frame. This is the\n    axis-aligned stand-in for \"find the main connected blob\" without pulling in scipy for one function.\n    \"\"\"\n    threshold = density.max() * frac_of_max\n    above = density > threshold\n    best_start = best_len = cur_start = cur_len = 0\n    for i, v in enumerate(above):\n        if v:\n            if cur_len == 0:\n                cur_start = i\n            cur_len += 1\n            if cur_len > best_len:\n                best_len, best_start = cur_len, cur_start\n        else:\n            cur_len = 0\n    return best_start, best_start + best_len",
  "new_string": "def _dense_extent(density: np.ndarray, frac_of_max: float = 0.3, min_run_frac: float = 0.02) -> tuple[int, int]:\n    \"\"\"Union bounding extent of every run where a 1-D density profile clears a fraction of its peak.\n\n    A plain min/max over every foreground pixel's coordinates breaks on scattered background noise\n    (a handful of outlier pixels near a far corner blow the bbox out to nearly the whole frame) -- fixed\n    by only counting *runs*, not individual pixels. ### User — 2026-09-15T06:44:48.320Z

**Tool result**
```
1	"""Redownload catalogue images from the source URLs already recorded in products.csv.
2	
3	`products.csv` (metadata only, ~1MB) is committed; the 7,272 images (~180MB) are gitignored per
4	`docs/PLAN.md`'s disk-budget note, so a fresh clone needs this to rebuild `data/catalogue/images/`
5	before an index can be built. Unlike `scrape.py` (which paginates each shop's live `/products.json`
6	feed at one request/second to be polite to the storefront's API), this only re-fetches static CDN
7	image files that are already known -- so it uses a small thread pool to make the README's <5-minute
8	clean-machine budget realistic. `--limit` builds a fast subset for a quick demo without the full
9	catalogue.
10	
11	Usage:
12	    python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images
13	    python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images --limit 300   # quick subset for a fast demo
14	"""
15	import argparse
16	import csv
17	from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
18	from pathlib import Path
19	
20	import requests
21	
22	from dyla_match.catalogue.scrape import USER_AGENT, download_and_resize
23	
24	
25	def main():
26	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
27	    ap.add_argument("--catalogue", default="data/catalogue")
28	    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=None, help="cap total images, for a quick demo subset")
29	    ap.add_argument("--workers", type=int, default=16)
30	    args = ap.parse_args()
31	
32	    catalogue_dir = Path(args.catalogue)
33	    with open(catalogue_dir / "products.csv") as f:
34	        rows = list(csv.DictReader(f))
35	    if args.limit:
36	        rows = rows[: args.limit]
37	
38	    session = requests.Session()
39	    session.headers["User-Agent"] = USER_AGENT
40	
41	    to_fetch = [(r["image_source_url"], catalogue_dir / r["local_path"]) for r in rows]
42	    already = sum(1 for _, dest in to_fetch if dest.exists())
43	    pending = [(url, dest) for url, dest in to_fetch if not dest.exists()]
44	    print(f"{len(to_fetch)} images needed, {already} already on disk, {len(pending)} to download ...")
45	
46	    ok, failed = 0, 0
47	    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers) as pool:
48	        futures = {pool.submit(download_and_resize, url, dest, session): dest for url, dest in pending}
49	        for i, fut in enumerate(as_completed(futures), 1):
50	            if fut.result():
51	                ok += 1
52	            else:
53	                failed += 1
54	                print(f"  failed: {futures[fut]}")
55	            if i % 500 == 0:
56	                print(f"  {i}/{len(pending)} done")
57	
58	    print(f"done: {ok} downloaded, {failed} failed, {already} already present.")
59	
60	
61	if __name__ == "__main__":
62	    main()
63	
```

68	def download_and_resize(url: str, dest: Path, session: requests.Session, retries: int = 2) -> bool:
69	    if dest.exists():
70	        return True
71	    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
72	        try:
73	            resp = session.get(url, timeout=30)
74	        except requests.exceptions.RequestException:
75	            if attempt == retries:
76	                return False
77	            time.sleep(1.0)
78	            continue
79	        if resp.status_code != 200:
80	            return False
81	        break
82	    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
83	    tmp = dest.with_suffix(".tmp")
84	    tmp.write_bytes(resp.content)
85	    try:
86	        img = Image.open(tmp).convert("RGB")
87	        img.thumbnail((MAX_IMAGE_SIDE, MAX_IMAGE_SIDE), Image.LANCZOS)

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/src/dyla_match/catalogue/scrape.py",
  "old_string": "    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    tmp = dest.with_suffix(\".tmp\")\n    tmp.write_bytes(resp.content)\n    try:\n        img = Image.open(tmp).convert(\"RGB\")\n        img.thumbnail((MAX_IMAGE_SIDE, MAX_IMAGE_SIDE), Image.LANCZOS)\n        img.save(dest, \"JPEG\", quality=90)\n    finally:\n        tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n    return True",
  "new_string": "    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    # Unique-per-call temp files (not a fixed dest.with_suffix(\".tmp\")) so two threads racing on the\n    # same dest (products.csv has ~1,500 duplicate local_path rows -- multiple product variants sharing\n    # one image) never read/write/unlink the same temp file. Final save goes through os.replace(), which\n    # is atomic on POSIX and Windows, so a process killed mid-save never leaves a truncated file at dest\n    # for a later run to mistake for \"already downloaded\" (the dest.exists() check above).\n    raw_tmp = Path(tempfile.mktemp(suffix=\".raw\", dir=dest.parent))\n    final_tmp = Path(tempfile.mktemp(suffix=\".jpg.tmp\", dir=dest.parent))\n    try:\n        raw_tmp.write_bytes(resp.content)\n        img = Image.open(raw_tmp).convert(\"RGB\")\n        img.thumbnail((MAX_IMAGE_SIDE, MAX_IMAGE_SIDE), Image.LANCZOS)\n        img.save(final_tmp, \"JPEG\", quality=90)\n        os.replace(final_tmp, dest)\n    finally:\n        raw_tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n        final_tmp.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n    return True"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "export async function matchPhoto(file: File): Promise<MatchResponse> {\n  const form = new FormData();\n  form.append(\"photo\", file);\n\n  let res: Response;\n  try {\n    res = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/match`, { method: \"POST\", body: form });\n  } catch {\n    throw new MatchError(\"Could not reach the matcher. It may still be starting up \u2014 try again in a moment.\");\n  }",
  "new_string": "// The backend runs on a free-tier host that sleeps when idle (docs/PLAN.md, \"Phase 6\") -- a cold start\n// reloads CLIP+torch+FAISS from scratch, which can take well over the ~1s a warm request needs. 90s\n// gives real cold starts room to finish while still eventually surfacing an error instead of freezing\n// the UI forever on a genuinely dead backend or dropped connection.\nconst REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 90_000;\n\nexport async function matchPhoto(file: File): Promise<MatchResponse> {\n  const form = new FormData();\n  form.append(\"photo\", file);\n\n  let res: Response;\n  try {\n    res = await fetch(`${API_BASE}/match`, {\n      method: \"POST\",\n      body: form,\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS),\n    });\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (e instanceof Error && e.name === \"TimeoutError\") {\n      throw new MatchError(\"The matcher is taking too long to respond \u2014 it may be waking up from being idle. Please try again in a moment.\");\n    }\n    throw new MatchError(\"Could not reach the matcher. It may still be starting up \u2014 try again in a moment.\");\n  }"
}
```

51	def far_frr_curve(
52	    scores: list[float], is_in_catalogue: list[bool], n_thresholds: int = 101
53	) -> list[tuple[float, float, float]]:
54	    """(threshold, FAR, FRR) at `n_thresholds` evenly spaced thresholds spanning the observed scores.
55	
56	    For the full trade-off curve `docs/PLAN.md` asks for under Refusal — not just the single
57	    operating point picked on the calibration split.
58	    """
59	    if not scores:
60	        raise ValueError("no scores")
61	    lo, hi = min(scores), max(scores)
62	    curve = []
63	    for i in range(n_thresholds):
64	        t = lo + (hi - lo) * i / (n_thresholds - 1)
65	        far, frr = far_frr(scores, is_in_catalogue, t)
66	        curve.append((t, far, frr))
67	    return curve
```

5	## Catalogue: what and why
6	
7	7,272 images across 1,010 catalogue entries: 400 Giva products + 607 Palmonas products (both scraped

12	are grouped by `design_group` so top-5 accuracy isn't punished for a same-design colour variant.

15	
16	Whole-image embedding (no fine-tuning: 1,010 products with a handful of views each would overfit

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:51:53.081Z

Now let's make the precision/recall macro-average logic explicit (not a bug — matches sklearn's `zero_division=0` convention — but worth being unambiguous):

---

### Claude — 2026-09-15T06:51:53.810Z

**Tool call — Read**
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/eval/metrics.py",
  "offset": 70,
  "limit": 25

### User — 2026-09-15T06:51:53.847Z

**Tool result**
```
70	
71	
72	def multiclass_precision_recall_f1(y_true: list[str], y_pred: list[str]) -> dict:
73	    """Per-class precision/recall/F1 for closed-set top-1 identification, plus macro averages.
74	
75	    Not a substitute for the FAR/FRR refusal curve above -- that curve needs a not-in-catalogue
76	    negative class, which this stumper set doesn't have (documented, zero-budget decision: no genuine
77	    negatives exist). This instead treats "which registered SKU is this a photo of" as a closed-set
78	    multi-class problem, which is answerable with the positives we do have and catches asymmetric
79	    confusion (e.g. one item's photos always being misread as another) that a single accuracy number
80	    would hide.
81	    """
82	    if len(y_true) != len(y_pred):
83	        raise ValueError("y_true and y_pred must be the same length")
84	    classes = sorted(set(y_true))
85	    per_class = {}
86	    for c in classes:
87	        tp = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t == c and p == c)
88	        fp = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t != c and p == c)
89	        fn = sum(1 for t, p in zip(y_true, y_pred) if t == c and p != c)
90	        precision = tp / (tp + fp) if (tp + fp) else float("nan")
91	        recall = tp / (tp + fn) if (tp + fn) else float("nan")
92	        f1 = (2 * precision * recall / (precision + recall)) if (precision + recall) and precision == precision and recall == recall and (precision + recall) > 0 else 0.0
93	        per_class[c] = {"n": sum(1 for t in y_true if t == c), "precision": precision, "recall": recall, "f1": f1}
94	    macro_p = sum(v["precision"] for v in per_class.values() if v["precision"] == v["precision"]) / len(classes)
```

**Tool call — Edit**

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/jaygautam/Desktop/dyla/eval/metrics.py",
  "old_string": "    macro_p = sum(v[\"precision\"] for v in per_class.values() if v[\"precision\"] == v[\"precision\"]) / len(classes)",
  "new_string": "    # macro average treats an undefined (NaN) precision/recall -- a class with zero predictions or zero\n    # true examples -- as contributing 0 to the sum while still counting in the denominator (len(classes)).\n    # That's the same convention as sklearn's zero_division=0 default, not an oversight: an audit flagged\n    # this as a possible divisor bug (2026-09-15, docs/ANTIGRAVITY_AUDIT.md) since it looked like NaN was\n    # being silently dropped from both sides; it's dropped from the sum only, which is the intended\n    # semantics, made explicit here rather than left implicit in the filter condition below.\n    macro_p = sum(v[\"precision\"] for v in per_class.values() if v[\"precision\"] == v[\"precision\"]) / len(classes)"

### Claude — 2026-09-15T08:05:35.813Z

Pushed. Here's where things stand:

## The audit was genuinely valuable
Most findings checked out. Two of them corrected conclusions from earlier the same day:
- **The verification re-rank wasn't as bad as I reported** — a real bug (double-cropping) made it look like 31.0%; fixed, it's actually 63.8%. Still below baseline, still doesn't fix the kada, but a fairer number.
- **The crop-to-object bug was worse than I knew** — it was bisecting rings/bangles into slivers. Fixed that too; crop recovers some ground but is still net-negative, rejected a third time on a corrected number.
- **The kada explanation was incomplete.** I verified it directly with real RGB measurements: the self-sourced items' "catalogue photos" are literally shot on the same home table as the query photos, so their high accuracy is partly background-matching, not pure SKU discrimination. The kada's real catalogue photo is studio-white — a genuine domain gap on top of the plain-jewellery-similarity problem I'd already named. `DECISIONS.md` now reflects this correctly.

I didn't take every claim at face value — a couple (the precision-averaging "bug," the Content-Type spoofing concern) checked out as already-correct or non-exploitable, and I said so rather than changing things that didn't need it.

## Also fixed for real
Several genuine production bugs: a clean-checkout build-breaker (self-sourced images were gitignored with no way to rebuild them), a thread-race in the image downloader, a `transformers` version fragility, an OOM risk in the upload endpoint, a path-traversal hardening, keyboard/screen-reader accessibility on the upload control, HEIC support for actual iPhone photos, and a few smaller robustness fixes. Full list in the commit and `docs/DECISION_LOG.md`.

## Where things stand now
- Backend + frontend: built, tested, all fixes verified working end-to-end.
- Deployment: still not done — waiting on you to create the free HF Space (steps from earlier still stand: huggingface.co → New Space → Docker SDK → free CPU → clone it locally → tell me the path).
- Everything committed and pushed.

Let me know once you've got that Space cloned, and I'll populate and push it.

**Credit Card Requirement:**
The credit card policy has evolved over time. In December 2022, Koyeb had to require a payment method for everyone due to an exponential surge of abuse, which was the quickest solution to control abuse and sustain the free tier. However, they now offer a signup that will prompt you for a credit card only if they find no automatic way to prove that you're a human. More recently, Koyeb is able to sustain the free tier without requiring users to put down a credit card.

**I do once you're set up:**

- Install Docker on it, deploy `backend/Dockerfile`

- Open the firewall/security-list rule for the port
- Verify it's reachable, give you the live URL

```
1	# Builds the matcher's data (catalogue images + FAISS index) at image-build time from the committed
2	# metadata, then serves it with uvicorn. Matches the README's "these are gitignored, rebuilt on demand"
3	# design -- applied to a container build instead of a local machine.
4	FROM python:3.11-slim
5	
6	WORKDIR /app
7	
8	RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends libgomp1 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
9	
10	COPY pyproject.toml ./
11	COPY src/ ./src/
12	COPY scripts/ ./scripts/
13	COPY configs/ ./configs/
14	COPY data/catalogue/products.csv ./data/catalogue/products.csv
15	# Self-sourced items have no image_source_url (nothing to scrape them from) and are committed instead --
16	# scripts/hydrate_catalogue_images.py can't fetch these, they must be copied directly.
17	COPY data/catalogue/images/own_*.jpg ./data/catalogue/images/
18	COPY backend/ ./backend/
19	
20	RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -e ".[ml,api]"
21	
22	# Bake the catalogue images and FAISS index into the image so the running container never needs
23	# network access to serve a request. This is the slow step (network-bound, one-time per build).
24	ENV KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK=TRUE
25	RUN python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images && \
26	    python -m dyla_match.cli build-index --config configs/default.yaml
27	
28	ENV CORS_ORIGINS="*"
29	EXPOSE 8000
30	CMD ["uvicorn", "backend.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]
31	
```
