1	# Dyla: Stump the Model
2	
3	A visual matcher for jewellery. Give it a phone photo of a piece and it returns the matching catalogue item
4	(top 5 with confidence), or says the piece isn't in the catalogue. The repo also includes a hand-shot set
5	of hard photos and an evaluation harness that breaks accuracy down by what made each photo hard.
6	
7	Built for the Thuli Studios (Dyla) take-home, Problem 2. CPU/MPS only — tested on an Apple M2, 8GB RAM.
8	
9	## Result, in one line
10	
11	CLIP (`openai/clip-vit-base-patch32`), plain whole-image cosine retrieval, no crop/re-rank/ensemble —
12	every one of those was tried and measured worse (see `DECISIONS.md`) — over a 7,272-image scraped
13	catalogue: **75.9% top-1 SKU accuracy** on 58 real hard phone photos of 3 items genuinely in the
14	catalogue (Wilson 95% CI [63.5%, 85.0%]). That number hides an important weakness: the one item that's a
15	genuine external catalogue match scores 0%. Full breakdown, rejected approaches, and where it fails:
16	`DECISIONS.md` and `eval/report_clip.md`.
17	
18	## Quickstart (under 5 minutes on a clean machine)
19	
20	```bash
21	git clone <this repo> && cd dyla
22	python3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
23	pip install -e ".[ml]"
24	
25	# Rebuild the ~180MB of catalogue images from the URLs already recorded in data/catalogue/products.csv
26	# (images are gitignored; the metadata + source URL for every image is committed). Full catalogue
27	# (7,272 images) takes a few minutes over the network -- use --limit for a fast subset demo.
28	python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images            # full catalogue
29	# python -m scripts.hydrate_catalogue_images --limit 500   # faster demo subset instead
30	
31	# Embed the catalogue and build the FAISS index (~1 minute on an M2)
32	export KMP_DUPLICATE_LIB_OK=TRUE   # faiss-cpu and torch both bundle OpenMP; macOS needs this set first
33	dyla-match build-index --config configs/default.yaml
34	
35	# Match one phone photo against the catalogue
36	dyla-match match --photo "data/stumper/photos/WhatsApp Image 2026-09-14 at 21.20.24.jpeg"
37	```
38	
39	Expected output: top-5 candidate products with cosine scores, and the own-catalogued gold chain
40	(`own-gold-chain`) at rank 1.
41	
42	## Evaluate on the hard-photo set
43	
44	```bash
45	python -m eval.harness --config configs/default.yaml --out eval/report_clip.md
46	```
47	
48	Reads `data/stumper/labels.csv` + `data/stumper/photos/`, runs every labelled photo through the matcher,
49	and writes top-1/5 accuracy (SKU + design level, Wilson 95% CI), per-condition breakdown, paired
50	clean-vs-hard drop, and per-item precision/recall/F1 — reported separately for the 58 real phone photos
51	and the synthetic augmentation used to pad the set past the brief's 100-photo minimum (see
52	"Stumper set" below). `eval/report*.md` in the repo are pre-generated for every backbone × preprocessing
53	combination that was actually measured.
54	
55	## Where things are
56	
57	| File | What it h
… [truncated 3815 chars]
```

```
1	# Plan
2	
3	Drafted 2026-09-14. Some choices are still open (see `STATUS.md`). Any change goes into `DECISION_LOG.md`
4	with the reason.
5	
6	## Phase 6: demo + deployment (added 2026-09-15, supersedes D4)
7	
8	Written down before any UI code exists, per instruction — nothing here should be guessed later.
9	
10	**Sequencing (my instruction, 2026-09-15):** model accuracy work first, this plan second, build/deploy
11	last — not started until told to.
12	
13	**Recommended stack, revised 2026-09-15 after "we can't buy any plan"** (Claude's recommendation; final
14	call is mine) — zero recurring cost, no card required anywhere:
15	- **Backend:** FastAPI wrapping the matcher, deployed on **Hugging Face Spaces (Docker SDK, free CPU
16	  tier)**. Originally recommended Render, but Render's free web-service tier (~512MB RAM) can't hold
17	  CLIP+torch without OOM-crashing, and a paid tier is off the table. HF Spaces' free CPU tier gives 16GB
18	  RAM, no credit card, and takes a plain Dockerfile directly — `backend/Dockerfile` reused as-is via
19	  `scripts/deploy_hf_space.sh`. Trade-off versus a paid host: still sleeps after inactivity, so the
20	  first request after idle time is slow (model reload) — acceptable for a take-home demo, not for real
21	  production traffic.
22	  - Google Cloud Run's free tier was considered and rejected for this: technically capable, but Google
23	    requires a billing account on file even for free-tier usage, which fails the "no card" constraint
24	    even though it wouldn't actually charge anything within the free allowance.
25	  - Cloudflare Workers was considered and rejected: doesn't run PyTorch at all. Using it would mean
26	    re-architecting onto Cloudflare's own hosted CLIP model (different embedding space, would need
27	    re-embedding the whole catalogue and re-measuring every number in `DECISIONS.md` from scratch) —
